### Next session: Needs Luis (in order)
1. **Phase 4, T2: native follower.** Luis joins and stands in the open. Claude runs `debug_run follower_test {action:"status"}`, then `{action:"summon"}`. Ask: did a Kalphite appear, and does it follow you? Then: leave and rejoin; is it still there and consistent? That is the gate.
2. If T2 fails, **T3: confuse.** Luis goes near any creature, and Claude runs `{action:"confuse"}`. Ask: does it start following you?
3. Further fallbacks are T4 (`AsyncDomSpawnAIAtLocation`) and T5 (raw `SpawnActor` plus `MoveTo`), following the plan in `docs/research/2026-10-25-dump-body.md`.
4. **Phase 3 gate:** teleport Luis with `K2_TeleportTo`, or give him a single harmless item with `InventoryComponent:AddItemByData`, then confirm it survives a rejoin. Luis's character profile is client-owned, so this also tests persistence. Use harmless items only, because the profile travels to the group's real world.
5. **Phase 5 `!ping` gate:** implement companion "say" (task P5.3) and test that the reply shows in chat.

### 2026-10-25: catalog run and melee combos (task P7.C1, in progress)
- **Catalog:** ran it live, staged, with no crash: `data/items_26161880.json` holds 466 items. It now records swing timing from the montage notifies (`--notifies`), and tools no longer count as "magic" weapons.
- **Generator:** `tools/gen_attack_data.py` produces `mod/body/attack_data.lua` (81 melee items, 26 sets).
- **Attack loop rewritten:** `mod/body/combat.lua` is a reactive behaviour tree on vendored beehive.lua (MIT).
  - It uses the held weapon's chain (Skullsplitter: Club 1→5) and issues each step at that step's input-window end.
  - It resets the chain after the finisher or after a long gap, and uses the weapon special between combos when charged.
  - It has `on_tick`/`evade`/`before_swing`/`after_swing` hooks for spells.
  - `skills.lua` keeps the handler contract.
- **Tests:** 30/30 Lua body tests and 56 pytest pass.
- **Next:** a server restart (skills.lua loads at startup), then Luis's in-game check: the mace chains and uses the special.

### 2026-10-25: spells wired + reflex rules (tasks P7.S1, P7.R1, P7.R2, in progress)
- **Body:** `main.lua` registers the spells service and the new `mod/body/rules.lua` in one block. Rules go first in the combat `on_tick` hooks, then the spells reflexes (shield on low HP or a telegraph, Windstep on 3+ clustered enemies).

- **Tests:** `mod/tests/test_rules.lua` (13), `test_spells.lua` (36), `tests/test_spells_rules_brain.py` (20). Full pytest: 113 passed. Lua: body 30/30, core 16/16, bridge 14/14.

## 2026-10-26 Director: M5 long self-directed session on the mock, measured
- brain/mock/sim_session.py runs one open Brynmoor session in simulated time:
  - game time is the clock; Haiku costs 2 s per call and Sonnet 4 s per request; polling every 0.25 s;
  - log rows use that clock, so tools/obs_tasks metrics work as they do live.
- The session goes chop → chest → 3 copper trips (60 copper in his chest) → plot → dig+plant → grinder+kiln → wait for the crop → harvest → flour → bread. It ends by choice after 504 s of simulated time.
- Biggest waste measured: waiting for the crop re-ran the Controller on every poll (58 runs; 99 Haiku calls in the session).
- Fix: a `wait` step field chosen by the model.
  - The board re-checks the test each tick with no Controller run.
  - It fails the attempt after WAIT_MAX_S (30 min) so the model replans.
  - It logs `board_wait_start`.
  - Result: 41 Haiku calls. Real Sonnet (3 requests) used wait:true for "Let the wheat grow" (cassette farm_and_process_crop).
- Gaps after the fix: p50 2.0 s, p90 2.25 s, all of it the Controller's first turn on each step. Idle is 26%, all of it the crop growing.
- Next gap lever, not built: let the Director give a step's first call, run before the Controller turn (needs an Agent.run_directed hook). Ordering work during waits is the model's choice.
- First calls (same session, simulated time; brain/mock/sim_session.py):
  - The Director's plan may give a step's `first_call`. It runs at once through Agent.run_first_call, which uses the Controller's own gates: offered tools, schema, safety.
  - When the step already passes on that call, there's no Controller turn, except on the job's first step or when something must be said.
  - Otherwise the Controller carries on from `first_call_result`.

  | | gap p50 | gap p90 | Haiku calls | Sonnet requests | wall |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | before (no wait, no first_call) | 2.0 s | 2.25 s | 99 | 4 | 504.5 s |
  | wait steps only | 2.0 s | 2.25 s | 41 | 4 | 504.2 s |
  | wait steps + first_call | 0.0 s | 2.0 s | 11 | 4 | 444.2 s |

  - Idle is 30%, all of it the crop growing; longest non-wait idle 0.25 s. Wait steps count as `step wait` in obs, not dead time (observability agent).

## 2026-10-26 22:48 → 2026-10-30 07:04: Codex period (summary, collapsed 2026-10-31)
Codex (gpt-6.1-sol + terra workers) ran the repo for 100 commits (7921ff5..6ee8375; no Claude trailers). The full per-entry log (≈1,500 lines) is in git at commit 41fa4cc; the audit is `docs/audit-2026-10-31/code_audit.md` (slices 1–4 beside it).
- **Volume:** +41k / −2.9k lines; about 9k production code, about 25k docs and tests; 47 docs-only commits. No GOAL target moved, no counted benchmark run, every goal audit read M1–M6 NOT MET.
- **Won live, each seen once, Haiku-led with developer-granted stock (dev world, ClaudeDev):**
  - self-care: ate, drank, recovered supplies from his own grave (10-27 20:29);
  - built a Crafting Table from a plain "cut down a tree" task (10-27 22:42);
  - table → Stone Logging Axe → equip → fell a tree; pickaxe → mine; built a missing Campfire and boiled water (10-28);
  - first model-chosen lodestone teleport (10-28);
  - one temple 200 m walking round trip on a route Codex had taught it (a907, 10-30); the repeat failed at 150 m.
- **Kept fixes:** speech channel (say-only), provinces/places data, FX guard, traversal profile + double-jump edge + `move_calib`, jump binding to the movement generation, arbiter mover reuse, service rebind on bridge adoption, `WaterItemData` water check, manifest args through dispatch, gather `mode`, lodestone NavigateBack map close, fresh state in follow-up prompts, `finish_reason` truncation check, `tool_choice=none` after research, 694fb84/aabdd66 (code stops overruling the model), live_queue / atomic_write.
- **Regressed:** go_to success fell from 56–58 % (10-25/26) to 14–21 % (10-28/30), mostly Codex's own refusals (`route_ended_before_goal`, `movement owner token required`, walk-guard rejections); every inventory read was marked "partial" with counts "at least"; prompts grew from about 7 KB to 83 KB; the `test_move.lua` suite grew to 232 s.
- **Root navigation cause found, never fixed:** the NPC navmesh does not cover the temple ramp, which a player walks; Codex added guard layers instead.
- **Self-inflicted limits:** Sonnet `max_tokens=2048`, a 20-calls/hour Director limit, about 10-minute full test gates with the client closed, and design → review → cold-review chains before any live try.
- **Facts learned:** DISCOVERIES "Codex-period facts, consolidated 2026-10-31" (items 1–29).
- **2026-10-31 response:** Codex paused; AGENTS.md deleted; CLAUDE.md §000 live-first working mode; Sonnet uncapped; clean-up on branch `cleanup-20261031` (navigation rollback to 19302df, guard/terrain/read-status/consume-TX/continuation removal, design-doc purge).

## 2026-10-31 Clean-up branch `cleanup-20261031` (offline; not yet live-tested)
- **Navigation:** move.lua, localplan, navmem and their tests are back at 19302df (0f192f2's `can_probe_hop` reapplied); walk_guard, sampled_ground_walk, walk_sense, floor_read, terrain and the floor/route probes are deleted. `test_move.lua` runs in about 43 s (was 232 s).
- **Route authority, new contract:** a missing oracle, `no_path`, a partial or ended route, or an exhausted local replan no longer refuses or holds 40 s. move.lua falls back to local planning (`route_fallback`) with a per-frame KillZ drop guard: a trace 1.5 m ahead; a drop ahead → local replan, then an honest `unreachable` ("no route and a drop ahead"). Design: `docs/design/navigation-route-authority.md` "Movement contract".
- **Owner tokens:** a brain go_to/follow first releases an orphaned non-reflex arbiter owner (`client_orphan_owner_released` event), instead of answering "movement owner token required". Reflexes keep the pawn.
- **Body skills:** inventory back to the plain slot loop (no "partial"/"at least"); `count_item` is one native call; consume uses one shared in-progress window instead of the TX; crafting plan_tree is baseline; lodestone says `not_unlocked` again; gather description restored (`mode` kept); `screen_job`, `capsule_feet_z` and the lexical hedges are gone; go_to arrival tolerance is 3.0 m again.
- **Brain:** controller loop is 8 turns or 300 s, no continuation slicing; one clipped result serialiser in the Director; checkpoint refusals are advisories (completion gate kept); full request logged on the first step only. Read-gap summary and review revalidation deleted.
- **Dropped:** the forward-memory route replay (b13793e) and route_feedback (2201844), both entangled with the deleted guards. Its whole-token `match_score` stays (it fixed "the" matching "gather").
- **Skipped:** the nav_oracle epoch/nonce simplification (it is the live wire protocol); the 2bff707 admission-layer removal (its refusals are now non-fatal through the fallback); child-generation owner binding.
- **Next (live, announced window):** `tools/client_smoke.ps1`; a far go_to (compare against 56 %); one gather; one craft; `inventory` text; consume water from the hotbar; one temple round trip; watch for `move: no admitted route` and drop-guard stops in client.log.

## 2026-11-16 (later): B16 placement speed: one-step aim, predicted view, stand spots; the batch house 3 in a row inside the bar
- **Result (dev world, debug client, the same 7-piece house: foundation, 3 walls, door-frame wall, roof on a wall top, door in the frame):**

  | state | 7 pieces | per piece | built |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | before (4ed87fe: swing, read the cursor, correct) | 95 s | 13.6 s | 7 of 7 once; door and roof usually failed |
  | one-step aim from the boom's pivot (9e76b76) | 36.6 s | 5.2 s | 7 of 7 |
  | + proof of the placement read 0.1 s after the press (was 0.5) | 34.6, 37.3 s | 4.9, 5.3 s | 7 of 7 |
  | + predicted view and stand spots (this commit) | **30.7, 27.8, 29.9 s** | **4.39, 3.97, 4.27 s** | **7 of 7, 3 in a row** |

  Luis's baseline is 3.81 s per piece; the bar (1.5x) is 5.7 s. Check phase per piece 0.4-0.8 s (was 1.8-7.8 s). Approach 7.7-10.6 s of the ~30 (was 12-15 s). He was exhausted (endurance 0, no sprint) in every run, so the walks are the slow case.
- **What changed (client_mod/scripts/camrig.lua, build.lua, deconstruct.lua):**
  - `camrig.look(rig, target, trace)`: what the screen centre would land on if he aimed at a point from a stand, BEFORE turning the camera (the boom's pivot, the arm pushed in by what is behind him, then the game's own line trace from the predicted camera). `build.W.stay_ok` uses it: place from where he stands when the predicted view lands on the aim point.
  - `camrig.stands(rig, feet, target, trace, opts)`: the candidates-in, feasible-out search. Spots on rings round the target (2.5, 4.0, 5.5 m), the ground traced at each, kept when the predicted view from there is clear and the spot isn't where the piece goes, sorted by the walk. `build` walks to the first (15 s cap, then the next, then the old rule).
  - Roof off a wall top: the view must land on the wall face on the roof's side within 0.2 m (`stay_ceiling_m`). The old loose rule (0.5 m + the wall's thickness) let a view 0.95 m off through and the preview came no nearer than 1.75 m.
  - `actor_check_s = 0.1`: the game confirms a placement 0.13-0.16 s after the press (materials spent, telemetry, xp, the new actor in the census: `data.verify_at_s`).
  - Per-piece timing in every build and deconstruct result (`phase_by_piece`, `stay_log`, `verify_at_s`), so the next slow phase shows without a trace run.
  - `deconstruct.W.aim` uses the same exact aim: 0.5-0.9 s per piece (one door-frame wall 3.7 s).
- **Tried and binned:**
  - Rotating 90 degrees with one input of axis value 6: the game turns 15 degrees per input whatever the value (the wall stood 75 degrees off). Six presses, one per tick, stay (~0.9 s).
  - `stay_m 6.0` without the stand search: one batch failed at #2 "can't get to a spot" (the site at x=132 was at the water's edge: client.log "entered water ... the goal is in it"), one took 87 s (the old stand rule sent him to a spot he couldn't reach for the roof; 59.9 s, then timeout).
- **Decision (mine, per "no shortcuts, real player code paths"):** he keeps placing through the player's own input path (build menu, cursor, place press). The MIT mods found by the research (WildPlans, RSDWCustomBuilds) call `BuildModeComponent:Server_SpawnBuilding` directly; that is the path Luis's own client sends (45 calls, as little as 0.57 s apart) and would cut a piece to under a second, but it skips the preview and its validity check, so it is not what a player's hands do. Not taken; noted for Luis as an option only.
- **Teardown of the same house (not a graded item):** 28.9-43.8 s for 6 pieces. The piece is gone 0.27-0.34 s after the press; then 1.1-1.4 s per piece waiting for the refund (it drops on the ground), and 1-18 s picking the refund up at the end.
- Tests: test_camrig 21 (7 new: look and stands against a toy world), test_build 153, test_deconstruct 86.
- Next on B16: the same look/stands service under pickups, gather and spell aim (they still aim from where the camera stands); the checker pressing place on a preview that isn't snapped; a full `tools.build_run --rung2` with the brain.

## 2026-11-16 (night): the first rung 2 run through the brain on the new aim failed (3 pieces in 233 s); four causes fixed (4bb4674)
- **Run 20261116-003814** (`tools.build_run run --confirm --rung2 --fresh`, brain log brain-live-20261116-013808-18780.jsonl, 5 Haiku + 3 Sonnet calls, $0.11): 233.4 s, foundation + door-frame wall + one wall. No roof, door, table, firepit, crate or storing. Judge: one false line.
- **What it showed, and the fix for each:**
  1. A stand spot counted as reached within 1.2 m of it, so he placed from where the view check had just said in_the_way / view_blocked: a red door-frame wall 0.30 m off its edge, a roof 1.4 m off. Fix: with a stand list, "there" is `W.stay_ok` true from where he stands (the predicted view lands on the aim point and he is not where the piece goes), read every 0.2 s while he walks; go_to tolerance 0.4 m; a walk that ends without a clear view goes to the next spot.
  2. A batch ended at its first failed piece: build call 1 stopped at #2, call 2 at the name "Roof", call 3 "Built 0 of 4" because the roof came first. Fix: a batch goes on past a failed piece. Only a failure that would meet every piece ends it (`M.BATCH_ENDS`: interrupted, cancelled, companion_down, no_permission, state_unavailable, body_error, lease_expired). Pieces that go on a failed piece are listed "Not tried". A blocked piece gets one more try only after something else has gone up.
  3. "got a shelter up and I'm resting in it now" was TRUE (rest rose 0 to 75); the false statement was the interact result "I can't get to the Magic Summon Shelter". By day the game's `time_of_day.sleeping` stays off when he lies in a shelter, so the press read as not having worked and the skill walked at it while rooted. Fix: `PlayerRestComponent.CurrentRestingAreaActor` changing counts as the press working (skills.lua `W.press_signals`); the result says he is lying in it and can't move until he gets up.
  4. He started exhausted (endurance 0: stamina capped at 40, no sprint), which is the harness's state, not his play. Fix: `build_run` tops water, food and rest before the line (`--vitals`, default 100).
- **Live after the fixes (debug client, flat ground at [118.5, 1911.5]):** the model's own piece list from that run (foundation; Wall With Door Frame #1:S; Wall #1:N, #1:E, #1:W; Door #2:in; 45° Roof #4:W) built 7 of 7 in 29.8 s (4.26 s per piece; approach 9.4, rotate 6.4, check 4.0, verify 3.6 s). Stay log: "placing from spot 1 (1.0 m from it, view 0.00 m off)". The roof went up before the W wall and still snapped. Shelter: cast, interact true in 0.9 s (changed: resting), get up ok.
- **Teardown in that run:** 64 s for 11 pieces. By phase: collect 17.4 (picking the refund up at the end), verify 16.7 (1.4-1.8 s per piece: gone 0.3 s after the press, then the wait for the refund to land), approach 10.7, aim 6.4, press 3.6, mode 3.1. The two waits are 34 of the 64 s.
- **Run 20261116-015416 died before the line:** started from PowerShell, where `sh` is not on the PATH (`all_pieces` shells out to tools/client_cmd.sh). Fixed: sh by its full path. Nothing was built or spent.
- Tests: test_build 154, test_camrig 21, test_deconstruct 86, test_placement 18, skills contract 10.
- Open from the run: the name "Roof" cost a call (the reply already lists "45° Roof"); teardown waits; the judge flagged a true line because the skill result it read was false (fixed at the source).

## 2026-11-16 (after the pause): the workflow change, offline only (docs/RETRO-2026-11-16-workflow.md)
- **Why:** rung 5 took about eight hours for one quest of four at its gate. 15 chain windows took 4.88 h of the body for 12 Rune Mysteries samples (24 minutes each); every failure was in part 2, which itself takes 79 to 209 s. Rates on the day: First Steps 12 of 12, Getting Started 5 of 13, Ratcatcher 9 of 13, Rune Mysteries 3 of 13. At 3 of 13 a streak of three takes about 104 trials.
- **Built:**
  - `tools/failure_ledger.py` (scan / tag / report) and `docs/failure_ledger.jsonl`: one row per failed run for the first thing that went wrong; seeded with the day's 23 failed runs, all tagged. Largest cause: six Rune Mysteries runs stopped with the next part open, fixed five separate times.
  - `tools/quest_clone.py keep / fork` and `park`: a checkpoint is a kept save; a fork is that save under a new name and guid; used forks are moved out of the character list after the window.
  - `tools/quest_run.py step` (K trials from a checkpoint in one lock window, `--until-step`, `--save-as`), `rates` (pass rate and streak per item, void runs left out), and a guard on `fresh` (the chain is refused until every quest passed 4 of its last 5; `--chain-anyway REASON` is recorded).
  - `CLAUDE.md` cut from 380 lines to 58 (original: `docs/BRIEF-ORIGINAL.md`); "How the work is run" in the goal doc; stuck triggers in the `search-before-building` skill.
- **Tested offline:** tests/test_quest_workflow.py (21), with test_quest_run, test_quest_clone and test_bench_devchar 45 passed; a fork of the real ClaudeQ161219 save, made in memory, differs from it only in guid and name.
- **Not tested live:** `step`, `fork`, `park`, `--save-as`, the warm-up wait and the quest flags. A fork does not restore the world (trees, NPCs, station contents, time of day), and the dev server keeps every forked character.
- **Next, when Luis lifts the pause:** one `step -k 1` window on a fork of ClaudeQ161219 to see that a fork loads with its quest state; read the Rune Mysteries flags there; keep it as checkpoint `rm_part2`; then K trials on part 2 and on Getting Started alone before any chain.

- **First thing that went wrong in c, d, e: the harness.** Trials a and b had mined the rocks. An essence rock grows back after 2 game days and only with no player within 100 m, and the forks play 29 m from them, so nothing grows back inside a window. Tagged `harness`, void (rule 3). gather's "used up" wording (c28130d) was seen live here, so run 155833 is tagged fixed.

Each trial's row carries what the clock did. Two tests (the hour wraps across midnight; a clock that did not move is said and does not raise). A dev-world fixture only.
- **Seen in the void trials, not fixed (they are his, in a world a real server will also show him when someone else has mined):**
  - After a true "none within 90 m, 8 used up" he asked gather again with a smaller radius (c, d).
  - In e the turn's three reads were spent (quests, look, look) before any action; told to act or reply he said what he needed and the request closed as done with the quest open. He did not go looking further and did not try the Anima Vents the step names.

**Fixes, each with a negative test that failed first. None tells him how to do a quest.**
- 5d07a74 (combat): a fight whose target has been out of reach for 2.5 s, or has taken 3 swings in a row without a hit, takes the nearest creature that attacked him, and goes back to the first when that one is dead (at most 8 swaps). The result names what else he fought. A creature seen dead is never the next target. Each swing is logged with distance, height and facing. Seen live in s8: the per-swing lines. Not seen live: a swap.
- f7aa614 (ledger): a passed run that a harness fault touched can be voided. Used for 215441.
- 1007076 (planner): the Planner's state carries the quest log with its map markers. From 222242, where the marker reached only the Controller. Tests only.
- 62d7fc7 (move): being in the water over a spot on a pond's bed is a verified arrival, and a failed arrival says how far short and which way. From 222242's fourth miss (2.4 m short over the pond). Tests only.
- 46c0ca9 (combat, defence): a block faces where the camera looks, so the camera goes on the one that swings; the plan counts the turn; the defence line carries his facing and the server's verdict on the block. 533 combat checks, 45 Lua suite tests. Tests only.

**Why 46c0ca9 and not the rule I had planned.** The research (`docs/research/2026-11-16-outnumbered-fights.md`) gave three rule changes: block instead of roll when the shield absorbs the whole hit, the neutral evade in a group, thin the group. Before building the first I counted what his blocks had done: block then hit 4 of 7, parry then hit 12 of 53, with timing that did not separate the hits from the saves. A live probe then showed the mechanism: the block key turns his body to the camera, and the fight code never pointed the camera at the attacker for a block (DISCOVERIES). A second probe showed the neutral evade carries him 2.15 m back, out of reach, so rule 2 was dropped. Rule 1 waits for the live numbers after this fix: the server's verdict now in the log line says whether a block that faced the attacker takes the hit whole.

The guard reads "chain: ready". By rule 6 no gate is tried until Ratcatcher alone is at about 9 of 10.

**A regression from 46c0ca9, found in s9's logs and fixed (d5f57ad).** After a block against an attacker that was not the fight's target, the camera stayed on that attacker. 8 swings went 84 to 168 degrees off the target and landed nothing. A live probe showed why: the attack key turns his body to the camera's yaw, as the block key does (DISCOVERIES). d5f57ad: in reach, the camera is on the one he swings at. With it, two things made readable: each hit on him says which damage event carried it, and an unanswered telegraph says how far off it was and the reach it was judged by. Negative tests failed first (4), 537 combat checks, 45 Lua suite tests. Not seen live yet.

**Found while reading for the next fix:** his combat memory is per character, so every trial's fork starts with none and learns each attack again (DISCOVERIES). That is where the unanswered Giant Rat lunges come from. Next: what he learns of a world's creatures is kept for the player, read by every character of his in that world.

Replay of that turn 40 times: `talk` 40, `give` 0. Live it is 3 of 21 `gs_start` trials. No change made.

**Found in s15's one failure, and fixed (13959e8, tests only).** After a talk that moved a quest on, the next observation still showed the old step and the old marker: 18 of 20 in s14 and s15, 35 of 47 earlier the same day (each talk result counted once; scratch `stale2.py`). `quests.lua` kept its `get_state` extras for 5 s and nothing dropped them when an action ended. So his first decision on every new step was made with the step in words (the talk result) but without the game's marker for it. In 024233 that was the look, the 62 s explore and the walk back. The bridge now tells the modules when an action ends, and quests and crafting drop what they kept. This changes what he is shown, not what he should do with it. Tests first (4 failing checks), then the fix: test_client_bridge 60, test_quests 27, test_crafting 228, the Lua suites through pytest 45.

- **Also seen, not yet looked into:** `look about rat` with `matched: false` returned rows named "Vannaka" for ash-log spawners and a lore item (`"name": "Vannaka", "seen_as": "Ash Logs"`).

All three failures ended the same way: he stopped with the quest's next step open and stood until the trial ended. The first wrong thing differed.

- **041914 (skill).** He called `start_goal`; a hunger run came before the goal started and was not told a job was waiting. It replanned the old goal, whose gather was retried 7 times in 3 s ("interrupted too often"). **Fix, 64dd510:** the situation of such a run says "Job queued (it starts by itself when this turn ends): <goal>".
- **044508 (skill).** He boiled 3 Clean Water, the stay-fed reflex drank 1 as they came out, and the bag check failed the craft: "+2, wanted +3". The failure note took the place of the note that names the open request, and he stopped. 7 of the 23 craft verification failures of 14 to 17 Nov were this (all Clean Water). **Fix, 7ce77d1:** what the reflex used of the output (the body's own `reflex_used` count) counts as made.
- **045210 (model, by the ledger's precedent).** The hand-in talk was a step of the plan. Its report ("New quest Rune Mysteries: Mine Rune Essence ... Given to you: 1 Stone Pickaxe") stayed on the task; the run that heard the goal end saw only the goal and "steps: 3", ate, drank and stopped. Since 15 Nov a hand-in made by the model's own call ended in a finished quest 7 times of 11; as a plan step, 2 of 12. **Fixes:** the goal's end carries what each step reported (64dd510); the talk report says the quest "is not finished: it moved on to its next step" in place of "is complete. New quest ..." (c3a0319); and a run that stops with nothing while a request a goal came from is open is asked once whether it is finished (64dd510, `STOP_FOR_NOTE`, logged as `stop_for_check`). The model decides at that turn; nothing tells it what the next step is.

**Offline evidence for the fixes** (each failed run rolled forward from its first Controller call after the goal's end, 10 times per variant, Haiku; the model's own replies kept). How often he went on with the quest:

| variant | 045210 | 044508 (craft counted as made) |
|---|---|---|
| as logged | 0 | 0 |
| step reports, old talk wording | 4 | not run |
| step reports, new talk wording | 9 | 3 |
| the same, with the one check turn | 10 (needed once) | 10 (needed 4 times) |
| step reports, finished "Job:" line removed | 6 | 9 |
| step reports and a closing tool `request_done` (old wording) | 5 went on, 5 closed as done | not run |

- **Binned:** the closing tool (it closed an open quest as done 5 of 10 times).
- **Left, queued:** the run that hears a goal end still sees "Job: <finished goal>" (removing it helped one run and hurt the other).
- **How often it happens:** on 16 and 17 Nov, 26 runs heard the end of a goal that came from a player's request; 5 ended with an empty reply, all 5 with the quest open (4 at "Mine Rune Essence", 1 at "Bring 50 Ash Logs to Zanik"). No run in the data ended silently with its request rightly finished, so the cost of the check turn there is covered by a unit test only.
- **Search (rule 5) and decision:** `docs/research/2026-11-17-stop-with-request-open.md`.

- **First miss, the attack.** He ran 82 m in 31 s and got within 7 m. A jump landed 139 cm from its point (130 allowed) on the right level, which counted as a missed landing. The new plan began at a spot he could not leave: five stuck checks on two aims moved him 0 to 3 cm in 8 s (`logs/client/client.log`, 07:36:02Z to 07:36:09Z). Wall-following freed him at 07:36:10Z. One second later the approach's 12 s no-progress window ended the attack.
- **The reason he gave was not true.** "The Giant Rat is higher up than I can jump" is said whenever the target was above jump reach at the start (24 m here). At the stall it was 4.8 m above him, and 6 s later his reflex's approach walked and jumped to it in 7 s.

- **How often.** 3 of the 74 attacks of 14 to 17 Nov ended `path_blocked`, all three on Giant Rats on this hill and all three with a missed jump landing. `go_to` failed as target_not_visible 2 times in the same days. The client log holds 128 missed landings since 16 Nov; of the 51 with a measured landing, 13 were on the point's level and within 2 m of it.

## 2026-11-17 08:10Z-08:55Z: the takeoff point he could not reach (fixed, seen firing once live); Ratcatcher alone, 5 of 5 (9 of the last 10)

**The cause behind run 073510 (Ratcatcher, the attack that gave up on the windmill hill).** Read from `logs/client/client.log`, 07:35:57Z to 07:36:11Z; the mechanism is in DISCOVERIES ("A local path's jump could start from a point his capsule cannot reach").
- A jump step took off only within 50 cm of its start. At a ledge's foot he stood on the ground 51 cm from it and could get no nearer. He pushed at it for 8 s; two re-plans gave the same path; the attack's 12 s no-progress window ended it one second after backtrack freed him.
- How often: 139 of the 4,297 stuck checks in the client log are of this shape (local path, aim 50 to 75 cm away, at most 5 cm moved).
- **Fix, c835589** (three tests that failed before it; Lua and places set 85 passed):
  - Within 75 cm of the jump's start, on the ground and no nearer for 0.6 s, he takes off from where he stands. The landing check is unchanged.
  - The stall reason is no longer the height he began under once he has climbed more than a jump's reach of it and was stopped by something since his last gain. He then says "something is in the way" in place of "higher up than I can jump".
  - A walk to a thing that is gone says "what I was walking to is no longer there". It is still a failed action (the grader's miss rule is not touched).
- **Not taken:** explore's "already in sight" as a success (3 right finds were counted as misses in 14 to 17 Nov). The matcher takes a thing whose whole name is in the words, so "rats or a windmill on the hill behind Vannaka" matches Vannaka; as a success that wrong find would be hidden. Queued until the matching is sound.

He ran back to point 21 off the ledge, three jumps from the foot missed, and the attack ended `path_blocked` after 29 s. Run 073510 shows the same run-back twice (07:35:50Z, 07:35:54Z).

- Sitting 2 of the same day failed (Ratcatcher, 2 misses; entry above). The streak counts from sitting 3.

**The reports of the 12 quest runs, judged for truth (new tool, 80bfb5b).**
- `tools/quest_truth.py` hands a recorded quest run's time window to the judge the build runs use (`truth_check` for the facts, `truth_judge` for the verdicts, Haiku, one call per run). It holds no quest knowledge: the context is the line sent, the game's quest log steps with their times, and the failed tool calls from the record.
- **Result:** 12 runs truthful, 0 claims flagged. Of 30 claims, 15 were plans ("on it, heading to Doric"), 14 were judged true and 1 unverifiable ("rats from the windmill up the hill": the windmill is in no fact). 12 calls, $0.009.
- **One "true" I do not accept as checked** (run 130059): "now mining rune essence from the geysers". The essence came from mining rocks (`BP_MiningRock_RuneEssence_Static_A_C`); the Anima Vent was a separate interact that gave the Astral Runes. The judge passed it because nothing in its facts names the source.
- **Limits of this judging.** The judge sees no bag (the record holds no inventory samples) and only body action results cut to 240 characters; nothing he read with `look`, `quests` or `lookup`. A quest has no single place, so the arrival rules never apply. One pass, variance not measured. It judges what he said, not what he left unsaid: a run ends when the game records the quest complete, so a closing line was often not spoken (125248 has none).

**Fix 1, 80bfb5b (bridge): `go_to` takes a thing in sight by its name.**
- **Failure:** `go_to` knew a thing by its id only. A name ("Campfire", "Rune Altar", or the name written with its id as a situation line shows it) went to the mover as a player's name and came back `unknown_target`: 4 of the 416 `go_to` calls of 14 to 17 Nov, each a miss (run 121459, with the fire 31 m off).
- **A second cost found in the same logs:** a walk to an NPC or station by its name started only after the body failed and the brain resolved the name: 298 such walks in two days, median 2.3 s each before the first step.
- **Change:** the bridge takes the id written in the words, else the nearest thing of exactly that name (letter case aside) among items, resources, stations and built pieces, and says in the result which one it took. Creatures and players stay the mover's.
- **Tests:** the new bridge test failed on the old bridge ("Workbench" went to the player's spot) and passes; bridge 61 of 61, Lua client suites and the Python bridge test 55 passed.
- **Not seen live yet** (window s31).

**Fix 2, 61412a5 (local planner): a slope up is read at its quarters.**
- **Measured first** (`logs/client/client.log`, 14 to 17 Nov, every planned local path against the `stuck` lines that name its point):

| edge | rise | planned | ended in a stuck |
|---|---|---|---|
| walk, up | under 40 cm | 2,304 | 45 (2 %) |
| walk, up | 40 to 80 cm | 582 | 93 (16 %) |
| walk, up | 80 cm or more | 137 | 70 (51 %) |
| walk, down | 80 cm or more | 40 | 0 |
| jump, up | 80 to 120 cm | 58 | 0 |
| jump, up | 120 cm or more | 164 | 15 (9 %) |

- **Mechanism (by the planner's code; the ground itself was not traced):** cells are 1 m apart, so a rise above his 45 cm step is a provisional "walk" and one trace at the midpoint decides slope or step. A rock with a shelf halfway up passes that test. This is the 2-minute reflex fight on rocks in s30 (brain log 134047).
- **Search (rule 5):** Recast tells a slope from a ledge by the rise between neighbouring cells (`walkableClimb`) and by the floor's angle (`walkableSlopeAngle`), on a grid much finer than 1 m. https://recastnav.com/group__recast.html
- **Change:** going up, the edge's floor is read at its quarters too. A part that rises more than his step height, or a floor too steep to stand on (the pawn's own WalkableFloorZ), makes the edge a jump. Edges going down are unchanged.
- **Tests:** two new planner tests failed before the change (a shelf halfway up; a face too steep to stand on) and pass; the 30 degree ramp and an even 40 degree rise are still walked; planner and move suites pass.
- **Cost:** 2 more traces for each rising slope edge of a plan.
- **Not seen live yet** (window s31). The measure is the same table for the windows after 13:56Z.

| measure | s29 + s30 (before) | s31 (after) |
|---|---|---|
| walk up, 80 cm or more: planned / stuck | 7 / 6 | 0 / 0 |
| walk up, 40 to 80 cm: planned / stuck | 38 / 6 (16 %) | 11 / 2 (18 %) |
| paths with a jump whose landing was judged missed | 8 of 43 | 11 of 16 |
| plan time, median | 0.72 s | 0.75 s |

- The change did what it was written to do: no steep walk was planned.
- The jumps got worse: 8 of 43 against 11 of 16 is p = 0.0005 by Fisher's exact test (`tools.ab two 8 43 11 16`). Paths inside one trial are not independent, so the true p is larger, but this is not noise. On 2026-11-17 I told Luis one window was too small to judge it; for the jumps that was wrong.
- **Reading, not yet shown:** an edge that used to be a steep walk is now a jump, and its landing is judged by the rule below. The cause sits in how a landing is judged, not in the slope check.

**Jump landings (new finding, not fixed).**
- **How often.** 14 to 17 Nov, before the change: 166 landings judged missed in 466 paths with a jump up (36 %). By edge: jump under 65 cm 12 of 30; 65 to 120 cm 33 of 77; 120 to 200 cm 48 of 153; drop-and-climb 120 to 200 cm 12 of 27; 200 cm or more 59 of 248.
- **The rule** (`client_mod/scripts/move.lua`, `steer_typed_jump`): a landing counts when he is within 130 cm of the jump's point (45 cm for a small hop) and within 65 cm of its height. Anything else is a strike, the edge is excluded and the path is planned again. In a fight's approach the third strike ends it as `unreachable: no_route`, which the attack reports as `path_blocked`.
- **What the misses were.** Of the 82 with a measured landing: 23 on the point's level and under 250 cm from it, 38 off its height but within the planner's own walkable slope from the point, 20 truly off.
- **One run in full** (s31 trial 1, the miss in the table): three landings judged missed (121 cm away and 69 cm off the height; 185 cm and 1 cm; 226 cm and 75 cm) while he closed from 29.4 m to 25.5 m. The attack ended `path_blocked`. The next walk reached the rat in 5 s.
- **What cannot be read yet** (rule 4): the log line gives absolute values only. It has no sign for the height, no takeoff position, no edge kind and no rise. That is the first change.
- **Idea, not built:** a strike only when he came down no nearer the jump's end than where he took off, as Unreal's and Detour's path following do (off the corridor after a link is a re-plan, not a failure count).

**Seen in s31 and not worked.** 14:16:15Z to 14:16:20Z: his reflex started a fight while a station menu was open. The input mode was `DIM_FocussedInGameUI`, the fight pressed back four times, ended `body_error`, started again and won. No miss, about 5 s lost.
